# 2A Are you a saver or a spender?

## A pairwork quiz activity

Sts ask each other questions to find out if they are savers or spenders. Copy one worksheet per student or pair.

### LANGUAGE

- Question forms in the simple present and the present perfect, money vocabulary
- What’s your attitude about saving money?
- Have you ever had a credit card?

- Put Sts in pairs. Give each student or pair a worksheet.
- Tell Sts they are going to do a quiz about money. Read aloud the first question and the three options. Demonstrate the activity by choosing one option and explaining why you chose it.

| Occupation          | How long have you been working or studying there? |
|---------------------|----------------------------------------------------|
| House               | How long have you been living there?               |
| Interests           | How long have you been doing it?                   |
| Sports              | How long have you been playing it?                 |
| Vacations           | How long have you been going there?                 |
| Social media        | How long have you been using it (or them)?         |
| Giving              | How long have you been helping them?               |
| TV                  | How long have you been watching it?                |
| Meeting friends     | How long have you been meeting there?              |
| Learning            | How long have you been learning it?                |

Get feedback for each card to find out who has been doing each activity the longest.

### EXTRA IDEA

Copy one worksheet per pair of Sts, and cut in half. Put Sts in pairs (preferably sitting face to face) and give them one half of the worksheet each. A asks B his/her questions. B answers, and then returns the questions, asking "What about you?" Then B asks A his/her questions. They find out between the two of them who has been doing each thing the longest.

When time is up, tell Sts to count their partner’s number of a, b, and c answers. The most common answer gives them their “type” in the text below the test. Sts tell each other what their relevant “score” is. Give Sts time to read the paragraph.

- Get feedback to find out if Sts agree with the quiz or not.

# 2B How long have you been…?

## A group activity

Sts play a card game to practice the present perfect continuous. Copy and cut up one worksheet per group of three or four Sts.

### LANGUAGE

- Present perfect continuous
- How long have you been living there?

- Write on the board: how long / ₜₒ my class? (come). Ask Sts to complete this question (How long have you been coming to my class?). Get Sts to answer the question.
- Put Sts in groups of three or four and give each group a set of cards, face down. Pick up the first card and demonstrate the activity by asking a student the questions. If necessary, write the incomplete question on the board and demonstrate, making it clear Sts need to complete the question in bold using the present perfect continuous.
- Set a time limit, e.g., ten minutes. Sts pick up a card and ask the questions to their group. When everyone has answered it, the next person picks up a card. Sts continue until they run out of cards or the time is up. Monitor to make sure the activity is being performed correctly.

### Answers

| A                     | Answers                                      |
|-----------------------|----------------------------------------------|
| English               | 1 harder, 2 the most difficult;             |
| Traveling             | 1 the worst, 2 more often;                  |
| Your life             | 1 more active, 2 unhealthiest;              |
| Friends and family    | 1 the best-looking (the most good-looking), 2 closest; |
| Technology            | 1 the best, 2 more practical                 |

| B                     | Answers                                      |
|-----------------------|----------------------------------------------|
| English               | 1 the most useful, 2 easier;                |
| Traveling             | 1 the longest, 2 more enjoyable;             |
| Your life             | 1 the most relaxing, 2 more healthily;      |
| Friends and family    | 1 best, 2 the most fluent;                   |
| Technology            | 1 more often, 2 the most expensive           |

- Now focus on b. Get Sts to sit face to face if possible to ask and answer their questions. They can either ask alternate questions, or A can interview B and they then switch roles. If there’s time, they could also return the questions, asking "What about you?"
- Get some feedback from various pairs.

# 3B Generally speaking

## A group discussion

Sts practice generalizing by discussing topics in small groups. Copy one worksheet per group of three or four Sts and cut up into cards.

### LANGUAGE
Not using the definite article for generalizing

- I think cats make the best pets because…

- Put Sts in groups of three or four. Try to have a mixture of genders where possible, as this will help to promote disagreement. Give out one set of cards to each group.

----

# 4B Tell us about…

## A group board game

Sts move around the board making sentences using have to, must, should. Copy one worksheet per group of three or four Sts. You also need one die per group and one token per student.

### LANGUAGE
have to, must, should

- You must not text when you’re driving.

- Put Sts in groups of three or four. Give each group the worksheet, a die, and enough tokens.

- and put them face down. If you don’t have enough dice, give each group a coin. Sts toss the coin for their turn and move 1 for heads and 2 for tails.

- Each player puts a token on the START triangle.

- Explain the rules of the game. Sts take turns throwing the die and moving their token. When they land on a circle, they must make a sentence following the instruction. The rest of the group has to decide if the sentence is correct and makes sense. Be the final judge in case of dispute.

- If the sentence is wrong, Sts move back a circle. If it’s correct, they stay on the circle.

- The first student to reach the FINISH square wins.

### Possible answers

1. You shouldn’t drink coffee / play on your computer / have a big dinner.
2. I have to go to the library / review for the test / do my homework / practice speaking English.
3. You must print your boarding pass / pack / check in / show your passport.
4. You don’t have to work / go to classes / get up early / do anything.
5. You shouldn’t go to bed late / worry / study too much / eat a lot.
6. You don’t have to pay cash / go to the store / try things on / carry bags home with you.
7. You must go at the right speed / stop at red traffic lights / let ambulances pass. You must not lose your concentration / text / eat / drink.
8. You should think about the price / why you need one / if you need a new one / any special offers and contracts.
9. I had to do my homework / set the table / share things with my brother or sister / go to school.
10. You have to get a job there / find somewhere to live / learn English.
11. You should find out about special customs / if you need a visa / changing currency.
12. You shouldn’t talk about religion / politics / money.
13. He (or she) has to correct homework / organize the classroom / prepare the class / prepare everything he (or she) needs.
14. You should be on time / bring a gift / thank your hosts for inviting you.
15. You shouldn’t exercise / go swimming / get cold.
16. A good student has to participate in class / do homework / go to class / pass exams.
17. You have to check in / go through security / go to your gate.
18. You should take an aspirin / lie down / close your eyes / drink water.
19. You have to be punctual / be nice to your work colleagues or classmates / listen to your boss or teacher.
20. You should be punctual / have information about the job / look professional. You shouldn’t get there late / talk too much / wear casual clothes.

----

# 4A Me and my opinions

## A pairwork speaking activity

Sts complete sentences about themselves and their opinions and share them with a partner to compare ideas. Copy one worksheet per pair and cut into A and B.

### LANGUAGE
Different forms of can / be able to

- I admire people who can play a musical instrument.

- Put Sts in pairs, A and B, and give out the worksheets.

- Focus on A and tell Sts to complete the eight sentences to make them true for them. The first four sentences are about themselves and the second four are about their opinions. Demonstrate if necessary, completing the first one to make it true for you. Set a time limit, e.g., five minutes. Monitor and help to make sure students are using the language correctly.

- Then focus on B and get Sts to read their sentences to Sts A. They should ask What about you? after each one. Sts then switch roles.

- Now Sts A and B read their MY OPINIONS sentences to each other, first A and then B. Sts should agree / disagree with their partner’s opinions and give reasons. Monitor and help.

- Get feedback from various pairs.

# 5A Marathon Cheats

## A Pairwork Activity

Sts read a text, answer questions, and use their answers to retell the story to their partner. Copy one worksheet per pair and cut into A and B.

### LANGUAGE
**Past Tenses**
- They had changed places several times.

- Put Sts in pairs, A and B, and give out the worksheets.
- Tell Sts they are going to read a story and answer some questions about it. Sts A and B have different stories. Give them time to read their stories in a and answer the questions in b. Monitor and help if necessary.
- Sts then cover their stories and use their answers to retell their story to their partner. Monitor to make sure Sts are doing the activity correctly.
- Get feedback by asking Sts to retell the stories.

### Example Answers:
**Student A**
1. The Comrades Marathon is a 55-mile ultramarathon.
2. On June 16, 1999, Sergio Motsoeneng finished ninth and won $1,000.
3. Nick Bester complained because he said that Sergio hadn’t passed him.
4. Sergio looked different in the two photos.
5. Sergio and his brother had changed places several times during the race.

**Student B**
1. On October 10, 2011, Rob Sloan finished third in the Kielder Marathon.
2. The other runners were surprised because they hadn’t seen Rob pass them.
3. The organizers thought it was suspicious that Rob had run the second half of the race quicker than the first half.
4. Rob confessed that he had gotten on a bus near the finish line and then jumped out of the crowd to run the final section of the race.
5. Steve Cairns was angry because he felt that Rob had stolen his moment of glory.

### EXTRA CHALLENGE
Get Sts to retell their partner’s story.
- Put Sts in groups of three or four. Give each group the worksheet, some tokens, and a coin. Each player puts their token on the START triangle.
- Explain the rules of the game. Sts take turns tossing the coin and moving one square for heads and two for tails. Sts answer the questions in the circles. Tell Sts that they have to give more information when answering each question.
- Sts play the game until the time runs out. Monitor and help. The first player to reach the FINISH square wins. If a group finishes very quickly, tell them to go around the board again.

# 6A Movie Quiz

## A Pairwork Quiz

Sts complete questions about the movies using the passive and then ask a partner the questions. Copy one worksheet per pair and cut into A and B.

### LANGUAGE
**Question Forms in the Passive**
- Which of these movies was set in the future?

- Put Sts in pairs, A and B, and give out the worksheets.
- Tell Sts to complete their questions using the correct passive tense of the verb in parentheses. Monitor and help.
- Check answers by eliciting the verbs only, not the whole question. You could copy the key onto the board.
- Get Sts to sit face to face if possible. Explain that A should ask his/her partner the first question and read the three alternative answers aloud. Make sure that they don’t see each other’s worksheet. If B chooses the correct option (the one in bold), he/she gets a point. Then B should ask his/her first question in the same way, and then Sts continue until they have asked all the questions.
- When Sts have finished, ask each pair who got the most points.

### Student A                   |  Student B
-------------------------------|-------------------------------
1. was directed                |  1. was, based
2. is based                    |  2. was shot
3. was dubbed                  |  3. is, set
4. was made                    |  4. were directed
5. was chosen                  |  5. was based
6. was nominated               |  6. was, introduced
7. was played                  |  7. has, been nominated
8. is set                      |  8. was composed

### EXTRA CHALLENGE
Get Sts to write some more quiz questions of their own.

# 5B Past and Present

## A Group Board Game

Sts move around the board answering questions with used to and usually. Copy one worksheet per group of three or four students. You also need one coin per group and one token per student.

### LANGUAGE
**Used to / Didn’t Use to**
- We used to visit my grandparents a lot.
- I didn’t use to get along with my brother.

# Current Page Text Extraction

## Instructions
- Give out the worksheets.
- Go through the list of jobs in a and make sure that Sts can remember the meaning of all of them. Then focus on the photos. Tell Sts that they are all real people who do one of these jobs. Sts work on their own and think about who might do which job.
- Tell Sts to use a pencil to write the symbols, so they can reuse the grids if they finish quickly.

## Possible Answers

### GRID 1
- I won’t move until I find somewhere else to live.
- When I finish this course, I’ll take an official exam.
- You’ll never get better at soccer unless you practice more.
- If I see Mark, I’ll tell him about the party.
- Unless they get here quickly, they’ll miss the start of the movie.
- As soon as you finish your coffee, we’ll go home.
- He won’t get that job if he doesn’t do well in the interview.
- After he gets here, we’ll have supper.
- Send me a text when you get home.

### GRID 2
- I’ll be at work until I finish this project.
- Unless you work really hard, you won’t get a good grade.
- I’ll call her as soon as I know the results.
- If you don’t stop looking at your phone, I’ll leave.
- After we have dinner, we’ll go for a walk.
- Tell her to send me an email when she finishes.
- I won’t buy a new laptop unless my laptop breaks.
- As soon as I get home this evening, we’ll sit down and talk.
- If you don’t pick me up after class, I’ll get a taxi.

### GRID 3
- After we choose where to go on vacation, I’ll book the hotel.
- If we don’t hurry, we’ll miss our train.
- We’ll look at other hotels before we book one.
- You won’t pass that exam unless you study hard this week.
- They’ll lend us the money as soon as we give them the paperwork.
- Give him my phone number if he asks for it.
- I’ll probably go shopping after we go to the movies.
- If Sally doesn’t come soon, we’ll go without her.
- I won’t leave until he’s asleep.

## Job Descriptions
1. Gloria de Piero is a British politician and journalist. She was first elected in 2010.
2. Richard Ward is an award-winning hair stylist to royalty and celebrities.
3. Sheku Kanneh-Mason is a musician and plays the cello. He won the BBC’s Young Musician of the Year award in 2016.
4. Ina Menzer is a German boxer. She was a World Champion boxer from 2005 to 2010.
5. Rachel Riley is a mathematician who studied at Oxford University. She’s also a TV host in a British quiz show, Countdown, where she does complicated calculations very fast.
6. John Cooper Clarke is a poet, who performs his poems in front of an audience and even records them and sells them, just like a musician.
7. Jessamyn Stanley is a yoga teacher who works to make yoga available to everyone.
8. Terry Hollands is a model for the clothing company Big Tall Order.

## 7A Three in a Row
- A pairwork or group activity
- Sts complete sentences using the first conditional to play Three in a row. Copy one worksheet per pair or small group.

### LANGUAGE
- First conditional and future time clauses + when, until, etc.
- I won’t move until I get a promotion.
- If I see Mark, I’ll tell him.

- Put Sts in pairs or small groups. Give each person in a pair, or each group, a symbol X or O.
- Give out the worksheets. Tell Sts that they are going to take turns choosing a square and completing the sentences in the grids. If they complete it correctly so it makes sense, they can write their symbol in the square. The goal is to get three in a row.
- Set a time limit. Sts play using all the grids until the time is up. Monitor to make sure that the game is being played correctly and to make the final judgement on Sts’ sentences.

# 8A I think…

## A pairwork activity

Sts complete sentences by guessing real information about their partner. Copy one worksheet per pair and cut into A and B.

### LANGUAGE
**Gerunds and infinitives**

**EXTRA SUPPORT** If you have an odd number of Sts, they can help A or B with the complaint. Pairing strong and weaker Sts for this would work well.

----

# 9A Sentence race

## A pairwork or group activity

Sts complete sentences using the third conditional. Copy one worksheet per pair or small group.

- I think you’re really good at math.

### LANGUAGE
**Third conditional**

Demonstrate the activity by writing on the board: when you go on vacation, you love… Elicit that Sts need to continue with a gerund. Then tell Sts to guess what you love doing on vacation. Elicit answers and then tell the class what you really love doing.

Put Sts in pairs, A and B, ideally face to face, and give out the worksheets. They must not look at what their partner writes. Tell them to complete the sentences, trying to guess how their partner would complete each sentence. Point out that many of the sentence stems could be completed with a noun, but Sts must complete them with a verb or verb phrase in either the gerund or the infinitive. Monitor and check that Sts are using gerunds and infinitives correctly.

When they are ready, Sts take turns reading their completed sentences to their partner, who tells them if they have guessed correctly or not. Encourage them to react by contradicting what their partner has said, and then giving the real answer if their guess is wrong, e.g., No, I don’t. I hate sunbathing! But I love walking / reading, etc.

Get feedback from various pairs and find out who, in each pair, had more correct guesses.

### Possible answers
1. We wouldn’t have been late if you hadn’t overslept.
2. You would have slept better if you hadn’t watched TV until really late.
3. If you’d told me you were vegan, I would have made you a salad.
4. We would have seen the movie if we had gotten to the movie theater on time.
5. If I’d had more money, I would have bought a better phone.
6. We would have picked you up at the airport if we’d known when your flight arrived.
7. If I hadn’t lost my wallet, I wouldn’t have had to renew my cards.
8. If we’d missed our train, we would have called you.
9. We wouldn’t have gone to that hotel if we’d read the reviews.
10. If our team had played better, we would have won.
11. If you’d used Google maps, you wouldn’t have gotten lost.
12. I wouldn’t have lent you that money if I’d needed it.
13. If you hadn’t left the door open, the cat wouldn’t have gotten out.
14. We would have bought that house if the bank had given us a mortgage.
15. If I’d known you were coming, I would have made a cake.
16. If you hadn’t reminded me of the appointment, I would have missed it.
17. We wouldn’t have been able to buy the car if we hadn’t asked my parents for a loan.
18. I would have left a tip at the restaurant if the waiter hadn’t been so unpleasant.

----

# 8B I want to speak to the manager

## A pairwork role-play activity

Sts role-play different situations. Copy one worksheet per pair and cut into A and B.

### LANGUAGE
**Reported speech**

I asked the salesperson if they would exchange it. He said (that) there were no more in my size.

Put Sts in pairs, A and B, and give out the worksheets. Tell them they are going to role play two situations. Tell them to read the first situation and prepare the information they will need, including what the person they spoke to told them. Give Sts time to do this and encourage them to write notes if they need to.

Sts then role-play the first situation. Monitor to make sure Sts are doing the activity properly. When they have finished the first role play, tell them to repeat the process for the second situation. Get feedback to find out who succeeded in their goals for the role play.

# 9B True or not?

## A pairwork speaking activity
Sts read sentences and then say if they are true for them or not, providing more information. Copy one worksheet per pair.

### LANGUAGE
**Quantifiers**
It’s too far for me to walk to school. I usually get the bus.

- Put Sts in pairs and give out the worksheets. Tell Sts to read the sentences and say if they are true for them or not. Demonstrate the activity if necessary. Read the first sentence on the worksheet aloud, "I usually drink plenty of water." Say if it’s true for you and give some extra information (I don’t really drink water because I prefer other drinks, like tea or soda.).
- Monitor to make sure Sts are doing the activity correctly.
- Encourage them to give more information. Get some feedback from various pairs.

## 10B Four little mysteries

### A pairwork activity
Sts read the mysteries and try to solve them. Copy one worksheet per pair.

### LANGUAGE
**Crime vocabulary**
- Put Sts in pairs and give out the worksheets. Explain that they’re going to solve some mysteries. Give Sts time to read the four stories and answer the questions.
- Monitor and help if necessary.
- Elicit ideas from various pairs and then give the correct answers.

1. They knew Sean was lying because he said he saw “a guy come up behind him," but the victim was shot in the stomach.
2. It was the maid. She said she was getting the mail, but there is no delivery on Sunday.
3. The waitress had told him she served him on their only plain plate. When she brought his “new plate of food,” it was on the same plate.
4. The assistant reminded the businesswoman that her key was safely locked inside the mailbox that it unlocks. After all, she mailed it there.

**EXTRA CHALLENGE**
Get Sts to write their own mysteries.

# 10A Relative clauses quiz

## A pairwork quiz
Sts complete questions using relative pronouns and then ask a partner the questions. Copy one worksheet per pair and cut into A and B.

### LANGUAGE
**Relative pronouns**
Can you tell me the sport that is played at Wimbledon?

- Put Sts in pairs, A and B, and give out the worksheets.
- Focus on A and give Sts time to complete the questions with a relative pronoun.
- When all Sts have completed their questions, check answers either orally or by writing the pronouns on the board.

**A**
Sports 1 that / which, 2 that / which;
Art and literature 1 who, 2 whose;
Movie and TV 1 who, 2 where;
Geography 1 that / which, 2 where;
Technology 1 whose, 2 where

**B**
Sports 1 where, 2 whose;
Art and literature 1 whose, 2 who;
Movies and TV 1 where, 2 who;
Geography 1 that / which, 2 where;
Technology 1 where, 2 that / which

**EXTRA CHALLENGE**
Get Sts to write their own mysteries.

# Review

## A pairwork speaking activity
Sts ask each other questions about key vocabulary areas, using a range of tenses and verb forms from the Student Book. This could either be used as a final “pre-test” review or as an oral exam. Copy and cut up one set of cards per pair.

### LANGUAGE
**Grammar and vocabulary of the book**
I’ve known Joe for 12 years.

- Put Sts in pairs. Give each pair a set of cards face down. Sts take turns picking up the top card and talking to their partner about the topic on the card, using the prompts.
- Tell Sts to keep their cards and then if there is time, to exchange cards with their partner.
- Encourage Sts to ask follow-up questions.
- Monitor, help, and correct if necessary.

**NON-CUT ALTERNATIVE**
Make one copy per pair. Give Sts a few minutes to read through the cards. Sts A choose a number for Sts B, who then talk about what’s on the card for that number. Sts continue taking turns choosing a topic for their partner to talk about.

**EXTRA CHALLENGE**
Get Sts to write some more quiz questions of their own.

## Person’s first name

### 1 Family

- Where / live?
- Who / live with?

### 2 Occupation / Studies
- What / do?
- Where / work or study?
- How long / worked (or studied) there?
- / like / job or studies? Why (not)?

### 3 Appearance and personality
- What / look like?

- What / like doing in / free time?
- / do any sports or exercise? Which?

### 5 Your relationship with this person
- How long / you known him (or her)?
- How often / see each other?

# 2A COMMUNICATIVE Are you a saver or a spender?

1. Do you usually have cash in your wallet?
- a. No, I always use my debit card. It’s so easy now with contactless payments; I can even pay for things with my phone.
- b. A little, yes. I’ve been in situations where I needed money and couldn’t use my cards.
- c. Always. I don’t like paying with cards; it’s much harder to know how much you’ve spent.

2. You’ve seen something you really love in a store, but it’s expensive. What do you do?
- a. I buy it without thinking about where I’ll get the money for it.
- b. I use some of the money I’ve saved and I buy it.
- c. I go home and see if I can find it on the internet for less money. If I can’t, I don’t buy it.

3. If you won or inherited money, what would you spend it on?
- a. I would spend it all on things I want, including gifts for others.
- b. I would spend some of the money, and I would put the rest in the bank.
- c. I would think of ways to invest it to get as much interest as possible.

4. What do you want your money situation to be in the future?
- a. I don’t really know. I never think about money.
- b. I want to have enough money to live on and make sure that I can buy whatever I want.
- c. I don’t want to have to worry about money when I’m older, so I’m saving now.

5. What’s your attitude about saving money?
- a. I think saving money is a bad idea. It’s better to spend it now!
- b. I know I should save money, but I haven’t always found saving easy. I do try, though!
- c. I save money without thinking about it. I am regular and consistent about it.

6. Have you ever borrowed money from friends or family?
- a. I’ve borrowed money quite often. I think it’s good to ask for help when you need it.
- b. I try not to borrow money because it’s easy to forget to pay it back. I hate owing people money.
- c. I hardly ever borrow money because I always have enough saved for emergencies.

7. How do you feel about lending money?
- a. I’m pretty generous and don’t worry too much about getting it back.
- b. I try never to lend money, especially to friends or family. It’s always ended badly for me.
- c. I don’t mind lending money at a good interest rate, if I get repaid on time.

8. Have you ever had a credit card?
- a. I use credit cards a lot. I never have enough money to pay for everything I want.
- b. Yes, I think they’re really useful. I don’t like being in debt, but if I can pay it back quickly, it’s OK.
- c. I don’t like using credit cards, so I don’t have one. I prefer paying by debit card or in cash.

9. When would you apply for a bank loan?
- a. If I wanted to go on vacation, or to buy something I really wanted.
- b. If there was a real emergency.
- c. If I had the chance to invest in a business, or some other investment opportunity, as long as it was safe.

----

**RESULTS:**

- **MOSTLY a** – You’re a **SPENDER!** You enjoy spending money and having new things. You aren’t very good at keeping track of your money, or saving. You’re probably very generous and like giving gifts to other people. You should probably try to plan a little more for the future. Try saving a small amount every week and put it in a bank account. You’ll see results in no time!

- **MOSTLY b** – You’re not a spender or a saver, you’re somewhere in the middle! You know that saving is important and you do what you can to make sure you save money, but you don’t spend too much time thinking about it. You don’t have a problem spending money on vacations or things that you think are important, but maybe you’re also afraid of taking risks with money.

- **MOSTLY c** – You’re a **SAVER!** You know exactly how much money you have in your bank account and you’re very careful with how you spend it. You want to make sure you have money for emergencies and in the future. You may know a lot about investments and which banks give you a good deal. Sometimes, though, it’s nice to spend money on gifts or vacations. You can afford them!

# 2B COMMUNICATIVE

## How long have you been…?

### OCCUPATION

- Where do you work or study?
- How long / work or study there?
- Do you enjoy your work or studies? Why (not)?
- What do you like most about your job or studies?

### HOUSE

- Do you live in a house or in an apartment?
- How long / live there?
- What’s it like?
- Would you like to move somewhere else? Where to?

### INTERESTS

- Do you have a special hobby? What is it?
- How long / do it?
- Where do you do it?
- Do you need any special equipment?
- Do you do it alone, or with other people?

### SPORT

- Do you play any sports or do exercise? What is it?
- How long / do it?
- Who do you do it with?
- How often do you do it?
- What do you like about it?

### VACATIONS

- Is there a place you often go to for a vacation?
- How long / go there?
- What’s special about this place?
- Who do you usually go there with?

### SOCIAL MEDIA

- Are you on Facebook, Twitter, Instagram, Pinterest, or any other kind of social media?
- How long / use it (or them)?
- What do you like doing or sharing on social media?
- How much time do you spend on social media sites or apps?

### GIVING

- Do you give money to a charity, e.g., Unicef, Greenpeace, Oxfam…?
- How long / help them?
- Have you ever done volunteer work? Did you enjoy it?
- Would you consider volunteering for a charity? For what kind?

### MEETING FRIENDS

- Is there a special place (cafe, restaurant) where you meet up with your friends?
- How long / meet there?
- Where is it?
- What’s special about it?

### LEARNING

- Are you learning anything new right now?
- How long / learn it?
- Why did you start learning it?
- Do you enjoy it? Why (not)?

# 3A COMMUNICATIVE

## Questionnaire

### Student A

a  Complete the questions with a comparative or superlative form of the word in parentheses.

1. What does get stuck mean?
2. Which do you think is **harder** to learn, your own language or English? (hard)
3. What’s the **most difficult** thing about studying English? (difficult)

#### Traveling

1. What’s the **worst** vacation you’ve ever had? (bad)
2. Which do you use **more often**, public transportation or a car? (often)

#### Your life

1. During a typical day, are you **more active** in the morning or in the afternoon? (active)
2. What’s your **most unhealthy** habit? (unhealthy)

#### Friends and family

1. Who’s the **best-looking** person in your family? (good-looking)
2. Who in your family are you **closest** to? (close)

#### Technology

1. If you have a technical problem, who’s the **best** person you know to help you? (good)
2. Which do you think is **more practical**, a laptop or a tablet? (practical)

b  Ask B your questions. Ask for more information.

----

### Student B

a  Complete the questions with a comparative or superlative form of the word in parentheses.

1. Apart from English, what do you think is the **most useful** foreign language to learn? (useful)
2. Do you find listening is **easier** than speaking? (easy)

#### Traveling

1. What’s the **longest** trip you’ve ever been on? (long)
2. Do you think traveling alone is **more enjoyable** than traveling with friends or family? (enjoyable)

#### Your life

1. Which part of the day is **most relaxing** for you? (relaxing)
2. Do you eat **more healthily** than in the past? (healthily)

#### Friends and family

1. What’s your **best** friend like? (good)
2. Who’s the **most fluent** English speaker in your family? (fluent)

#### Technology

1. Which do you replace **more often**, your phone or your laptop/tablet? (often)
2. What’s the **most expensive** gadget you’ve ever bought? (expensive)

b  Ask A your questions. Ask for more information.

# 5A COMMUNICATIVE Marathon cheats

## Student A Seeing double

### a Read the story.
The Comrades Marathon is an ultramarathon in which people run 55 miles. It takes place in South Africa and it’s the oldest and most famous ultramarathon. People who run it can win a lot of money, so a lot of people compete every year. On June 16, 1999, a young man named Sergio Motsoeneng finished 9th and he got $1,000 prize money. But Nick Bester, who finished in 15ᵗʰ place, said that he hadn’t seen Sergio pass him. The officials checked that Sergio had been at all the stops and lots of people had seen him. It seemed that everything was OK, until a newspaper published two photos of Sergio. In the first photo, Sergio was wearing a yellow watch on his left wrist, but in the second photo he was wearing a pink watch on his right wrist. His legs looked different, too. Sergio had gone to a bathroom after 45 minutes of running and changed places with his brother, Fika, who was waiting for him inside. They had changed places several times during the marathon. Both brothers were disqualified.

### b Now answer these questions.
1. What is the Comrades Marathon?
2. What happened on June 16, 1999?
3. Why did Nick Bester complain?
4. What was wrong with the photos of Sergio?
5. Why were the brothers disqualified?

### c Cover the text and look at your questions and answers. Tell B your story.

### d Now listen to B’s story.

## Student B The bus ride

### a Read the story.
On October 10, 2011, Rob Sloan won third place at the Kielder Marathon. He completed the 26.2 mile race in an impressive time of two hours and 51 minutes. The other runners were surprised because they didn’t remember seeing Rob pass them, especially Steve Cairns, who had finished in fourth place.

### b Now answer these questions.
1. What happened on October 10, 2011?
2. Why were the other runners surprised?
3. What did the organizers think was suspicious?
4. What did Rob Sloan confess?
5. Why was Steve Cairns angry?

### c Listen to A’s story.
When they asked Rob, he was very angry and said that he had run the whole marathon. The organizers checked the times and realized that Rob had run the second half of the marathon quicker than the first half. This was really suspicious. Some spectators also said that they had seen Rob get off a bus near the finish line. Rob confessed that he had decided to leave the race after 20 miles because he was feeling very tired. But then he had seen a bus that was taking tourists to the finish line and got on it. He then jumped out of the crowd near the finish line and ran the final section of the marathon. The marathon organizers took his medal away and gave it to Steve Cairns, the real third place winner. Steve Cairns was still really angry because he felt that Rob Sloan had stolen his moment of glory.

### d Now cover the text and look at your questions and answers. Tell A your story.

1. Which of these movies **(direct)** by Steven Spielberg?
- a) Jurassic World
- b) The Return of the Jedi
- c) Indiana Jones and the Last Crusade

2. Which of these movies **(base)** on a true event?
- a) Battle of the Sexes
- b) Murder on the Orient Express
- c) La La Land

- a) Mulan
- b) Hercules
- c) Toy Story

# COMMUNICATIVE

## If you had to choose…

Talk to a partner. Explain your answers.

- I’d prefer to live in a new apartment, because if I lived in an old apartment, I’d have to do a lot of work on it.

### If you had to choose…

- Would you prefer to live in a new apartment, or in an old apartment that you could renovate yourself?

- Would you prefer to have a vacation house on the coast or in the mountains?

- Would you prefer to have a house with solar energy or a house with traditional electricity?

- If you had space in your house, would you prefer to have a gym or a game room?

- Would you prefer to live in a four-bedroom house in the country or a one-bedroom apartment downtown?

- Would you prefer to live in a house next to an elementary school or next to a restaurant?

- Would you prefer to live in a modern, spacious apartment or in an old, cozy house?

# COMMUNICATIVE

## I want to speak to the manager

### Student A

a. Look at the situations. Spend a few minutes preparing what you’re going to say.

**Situation 1**
You’re a customer in a clothing store. You bought something in a clothing store on sale yesterday (decide what) and there’s a problem (decide what). Go back to the store. You’ve spoken to a salesperson, but she didn’t help you (decide what she said). B is the manager. You’d like to exchange your item for another identical one. If you can’t, you’d like a refund. You should also complain about what the salesperson told you.

**Situation 2**
You’re the manager of a restaurant. Your usual chef is off this week and you have a temporary chef who is not very good. One of the waiters has a problem with a customer, who would like to speak to you. When customers complain, you usually offer them a free drink or a coffee. If it’s absolutely necessary, you might give a 10% discount on their bill, but you would prefer not to. B is the customer.

b. Role-play situation 1. You start.
*Excuse me, I bought…*

c. Now role-play situation 2. B will start.

### Student B

a. Look at the situations. Spend a few minutes preparing what you’re going to say.

**Situation 1**
You’re the manager of a clothing store. A is a customer. He / She is going to come to you with a problem with something he / she bought on sale yesterday. A has spoken to a salesperson. You can’t exchange A’s item for an identical one because there are no more in his / her size. Try to persuade A to exchange it for something else, because you don’t usually give refunds during sales.

**Situation 2**
You’re a customer in a restaurant. You’ve just finished your meal (decide what you had) and you didn’t enjoy it at all (decide what was wrong with it). You’ve complained to the waiter, but he didn’t solve the problem (decide what he said). You’ve asked the waiter to call the manager. Try to get at least a 50% discount on your meal. You should also complain about what the waiter told you. A is the manager.

b. Role-play situation 1. A will start.

c. Now role-play situation 2. You start.
*Good evening. Are you the manager?*

### Complete the questions with who, that, which, where, or whose.
- Ask B the questions. (Begin with Can you tell me…?)
- If B knows the answer, he / she gets three points.
- If B doesn’t know the answer, give him / her the three options.
- If B gets it right, he / she gets one point.

### Complete the questions with who, that, which, where, or whose.
- Ask A the questions. (Begin with Can you tell me…?)
- If A knows the answer, he / she gets three points.
- If A doesn’t know the answer, give him / her the three options.
- If A gets it right, he / she gets one point.

# 10B  COMMUNICATIVE Four little mysteries

Read the four mysteries. With your partner, try to answer the questions. Give your reasons.

## 1. Baggy suit
A crime happened on Freemont Street. The main suspect is a man named Sean Baker. A man had been walking along the street when somebody shot him in the stomach. The suspect had brown hair, blue eyes, and wore a baggy suit, just like Sean Baker’s. Sean was asked to tell the story right from the beginning. “Well,” said Sean, “I was just hanging around the park when I saw this man walking along the street. Suddenly, a guy came up behind him and shot him! I ran home as fast as I could.” The police officers asked him to give a description of the murderer. Sean said, “He had a red mustache and red hair, and he was wearing a baggy Armani suit.” “I think this man is telling a lie,” said one of the police officers. How did he know?

## 2. Sunday murder
A man was found murdered on Sunday morning. His wife immediately called the police. The police questioned the wife and staff and got these answers:
- The wife said she was sleeping.
- The cook said she was preparing breakfast.
- The gardener said he was gathering vegetables.
- The maid said she was getting the mail.

The police instantly arrested the murderer. Who did it and how did they know?

## 3. There’s an insect on my plate!
A man was eating at a restaurant. When his food came, the server told him that they had run out of their usual elegant dinner plates and had to serve his meal on their only plain plate. The man didn’t mind, but there was an insect in his food. The server took it away and said she would bring him a new plate of food. When she came back, the man knew she had only thrown away the insect. How did he know?

## 4. You have mail
A businesswoman went away on a long business trip. Before she left, she asked her assistant to forward all of her mail. However, she forgot to give the assistant the key to her mailbox. When she realized her mistake, she mailed the key to her office, so the assistant could send her mail to her. But the assistant never did. The businesswoman got angry and called her office, planning to fire her assistant. However, the assistant was able to explain why she hadn’t sent the mail. The businesswoman quickly realized her mistake and apologized. What did the assistant say to make the businesswoman apologize?

# COMMUNICATIVE REVIEW

## 1 Food and cooking
Talk for one minute about what and where you eat.
- Do you think you have a healthy diet? Why (not)?
- Are you trying to cut down on anything right now?
- Do you prefer eating out or eating at home? Why?
- What’s your favorite dish when you eat out?

## 2 Family and friends
Describe a member of your family.
- What does he / she look like?
- What is he / she like? (Give two positive and two negative characteristics.)
- In what ways are you similar or different?
- How well do you get along with this person?

## 3 Money
Tell your partner about your attitude to money.
- Are you careful with money? How?
- Do you usually leave tips in cafes, taxis, or restaurants? Why (not)?
- Is there anything you don’t like spending money on? Why (not?)
- Do you think credit cards are a good thing? Why (not?)

## 4 Transportation
Talk to your partner about transportation in your town.
- What’s your favorite form of transportation? Why?
- What’s the best and worst way to get around your town / city?
- How do you get to the place where you work / study?
- If you could change one thing about transportation in your town, what would it be?

## 5 Sports
Tell your partner about…
- a sport you really like watching or doing.
- a sport you used to do and why you stopped.
- a team you support. How long have you been supporting them? Do you go and see them play?
- the sporting event you most remember.

## 6 Relationships
Tell your partner about a good friend of yours.
- How long have you known each other?
- How and where did you meet?
- Why do you get along well together?
- Have you ever had a serious argument?
- How often do you see each other?

## 7 The movies
Think of a movie (but don’t say the name). Describe it for your partner to guess.
- Where is it set?
- Is it based on a book?
- Who is it directed by?
- Who is in it?
- What is it about?

## 8 Your education
Tell your partner about your high school (or elementary school if you are still in school).
- Did you use to wear a uniform?
- What subjects were you good at / bad at?
- Did you use to have a favorite teacher?
- Were there any teachers you didn’t like? Why (not)?

## 9 Your home
Talk about your home.
- Where do you live?
- How long have you lived there?
- What kind of house or apartment is it? Can you describe it?
- What do you like about it and the area where you live? What don’t you like?

## 10 Work
Think of a friend or family member who has a job. Tell your partner about the good and bad side of his / her job. Talk about…
- the salary.
- the hours.
- the vacations.
- the best and worst thing about the job.

# Vocabulary Activity Instructions

## Introduction Classroom Language
### A Review Activity
Sts review classroom language. Copy one worksheet per student.

### LANGUAGE
Phrases and questions to use in class
- Give each student a worksheet. Focus on each section and elicit/explain the meaning and drill the pronunciation of each question.
- If Sts wish, they can write a translation of each line alongside it. You could get Sts to test themselves or each other by covering each line and looking at the translation.
- Tell Sts that these are questions that you expect Sts to always use in English, and be strict about not letting them say them in their L1.

**EXTRA IDEA**: You could copy and enlarge this sheet and put it up in the classroom to help Sts.

----

## 1A Food and Cooking
### A Card Game
Sts define words/phrases for other Sts to guess. Copy and cut up one set of cards per pair or small group.

### LANGUAGE
Words associated with food and cooking
- Put Sts in pairs or small groups. Give each pair/group a set of cards face down, or in an envelope.
- Demonstrate the activity. Choose another word (not one of the ones on the cards) from the Vocabulary Bank Food and cooking p.152. Describe it, e.g., It’s meat that comes from a cow, until a student guesses the word (beef). Highlight that Sts are not allowed to use the word(s) on the card in their definition.
- Sts put the cards face down. They play the game, taking turns picking up a card and describing the word/phrase. Sts describing the word/phrase shouldn’t let their partners see what’s on the card. Tell Sts to wait until their partner has finished his/her description before trying to guess the word.

**EXTRA IDEA**: If Sts are playing in groups, the student who correctly guesses the word first gets the card. The student with the most cards at the end of the game wins.

**NON-CUT ALTERNATIVE**: Put Sts in pairs. Copy one sheet per pair and fold it down the middle. Sts look at one half each, and take turns describing the words/phrases to their partners until they guess the correct answer.

----

## 1B Personality
### An Information Gap Activity
Sts define words to help their partner complete a crossword. Copy one worksheet per pair and cut into A and B.

### LANGUAGE
Personality adjectives
- Put Sts in pairs, ideally face to face, and give out the crosswords. Make sure that Sts can’t see each other’s worksheets. Explain that A and B have the same crossword, but with different words missing. They have to define words to each other to complete their crosswords.
- Give Sts a minute to read their instructions. If Sts don’t know what a word means, they can look it up in Vocabulary Bank Personality p.153. Make sure Sts understand the difference between across and down. Remind them that they can’t use any part of the word in their definition.
- Sts take turns asking each other for their missing words (e.g., What’s 1 down? What’s 3 across?). Their partner must define/describe the word until the other student is able to write it in his/her crossword. Sts should help each other with clues if necessary.
- When Sts have finished, they should compare their crosswords to make sure they have the same words and have spelled them correctly.
- Focus on Activation. Sts tell each other the definitions of the words they wrote in their crossword. Monitor and help.

----

## 2A Money
### A Pairwork Vocabulary Race
Sts complete sentences. Copy one worksheet per pair.

### LANGUAGE
Words associated with money
- Put Sts in pairs and give out the worksheets. Focus on the instructions. Tell Sts that they have to fill in as many blanks as they can in three minutes. Tell them to write in the MISSING WORDS column.
- Check answers. The first pair to complete all the phrases correctly wins, or the pair with the most correct answers at the end of the time limit wins.

| 2  | worth             |
|----|-------------------|
| 3  | live off          |
| 4  | pay (very much) for|
| 5  | earn              |
| 6  | pay back          |
| 7  | bill              |
| 8  | from              |
| 9  | wasting           |
| 10 | save              |
| 11 | pay by           |
| 12 | lend              |
| 13 | debt              |
| 14 | bill              |
| 15 | inherited         |
| 16 | charge            |
| 17 | ATM               |
| 18 | mortgage          |
| 19 | tax               |
| 20 | account           |

- Focus on Activation. Get Sts to cover the MISSING WORDS column and read the sentences with the missing words aloud. They can test each other or themselves.

**EXTRA IDEA**: You could get Sts to do the exercise individually and compare their answers with a partner. Then check answers.

# 3A Transportation

## A fill-in-the-blank activity
Sts look at pictures and write the words. Copy one worksheet per student.

### LANGUAGE
- Focus on Activation. Tell Sts to cover the column on the right and see if they can remember the missing words.

**EXTRA IDEA**: Put Sts in pairs, A and B. Sts A (looking at the worksheet) read clues 1–10 to Sts B (worksheet covered) who say the word. Sts then switch roles for clues 11–20.

- Words associated with transportation

Give each student a worksheet and ask them to write the noun or compound noun for the things they see in the pictures. Check answers.

- Words associated with relationships, simple past

Give Sts time to read the stories and complete them with the simple past of the verbs in each of the lists. Monitor and check that they are completing the stories correctly. Check answers, eliciting the verbs onto the board.

**My best friend at school**

# 6A Movies

## A fill-in-the-blank activity
Sts complete movie reviews. Copy one worksheet per student.

### LANGUAGE
- Words associated with the movies

Give out the worksheets. Set a time limit, e.g., five minutes. Tell Sts they have to read the movie reviews and complete them with the correct noun or compound noun in either the singular or plural. Point out that the first letter or two is given. Monitor to make sure they are completing the movie reviews correctly.

| No. | Word                     |
|-----|--------------------------|
| 1   | extras                   |
| 2   | star                     |
| 3   | soundtrack               |
| 4   | plot                     |
| 5   | trailer                  |
| 6   | action movie             |
| 7   | special effects          |
| 8   | script                   |
| 9   | scene                    |

# 6B The Body

**Be punished** is to be made to suffer because you’ve done something bad or wrong.
**Be suspended** is to have to leave school for a certain amount of time (usually for a day up to a week) because you’ve done something wrong.
**Cheat** is to act in a dishonest way in order to gain an advantage in an exam.
**Misbehave** is to behave badly.
**A public school** is run by the government and is free. You have to pay to go to a **private school**.
**A principal** is a person who is in charge of a school.
**A teacher** teaches in any school except a university.
**Make somebody study something** is to force somebody to study.
**Let somebody study something** is to give somebody permission or an opportunity to study.
**A graduate** is a person who has a university degree.
**A student** is a person who studies at any level.
**A nursery school** is a school for children between the ages of two and four in the US. It is not compulsory.
**An elementary school** is a school for children between the ages of five and ten in the US. It is compulsory.
**History** is a subject in school or college that is the study of past events.
**A story** is a description of events and people that a writer has invented in order to entertain.

## A Fill-in-the-Blank Activity

Sts write the verb and name the part(s) of the body shown in each photo. Copy one worksheet per student.

### LANGUAGE
**Verbs associated with the body**

- Give each student a worksheet and ask them to write next to each photo the verb and the noun (singular or plural) for the part of the body marked with an arrow. Check answers.

| Number | Verb   | Noun     |
|--------|--------|----------|
| 1      | throw  | back     |
| 2      | clap   | hand     |
| 3      | whistle| lips     |
| 4      | stare  | eye      |
| 5      | bite   | teeth    |
| 6      | kick   | leg      |
| 7      | taste  | tongue   |
| 8      | smile  | mouth    |
| 9      | point  | finger   |
| 10     | touch  | thumb    |
| 11     | smell  | nose     |

- Focus on Activation. Put Sts in pairs and get them to say what other parts of the body they can see in the photos (e.g., shoulder, knee, etc.).
- Get some feedback.

# 7A Education

## A Team Game

Sts explain the difference between two words/phrases. Copy and cut up one set of cards.

### LANGUAGE
**Words associated with education**

- Divide the class into two teams (or more if you have a lot of Sts).
- Give a card to each team. Give Sts a minute to decide what the difference is between the two words or phrases.
- Write the two words or phrases on each team’s card on the board.
- A spokesperson from each team takes turns explaining the difference to the rest of the class. If the explanation is correct, the team gets a point. If it isn’t correct, the other team can try to win an extra point by explaining the difference correctly before taking their own turn.
- Then give each team another card. Keep a record of each team’s points on the board. The team with the most points wins.

| Term                | Definition                                                                 |
|---------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| A high school       | is for children aged between 11–18 years in the US.                      |
| A secondary school   | is for children aged between 11–18 years in the UK.                      |
| A child             | is someone under the age of 18 who may or may not study in school.      |
| A student           | is a child or adult who goes to school at any level.                     |
| A term              | is one of the three periods of the year during which classes are held in schools and universities in the UK. |
| A semester          | is the two periods that the school/college year is divided into in the US. |
| Pass an exam        | is to achieve the required standard in an exam or test.                  |
| Fail an exam        | is the opposite.                                                          |

Sts complete the descriptions of three different homes. Copy one worksheet per student.

### LANGUAGE
**Words associated with houses**

- Give out the worksheets. Set a time limit, e.g., three minutes. Tell Sts to read the house descriptions and complete them with the correct words. Point out that the first letter or two is given.
- Monitor to make sure they are completing the descriptions correctly.
- Check answers.

| 13     | second floor     |
| 14     | basement         |

- Focus on Activation. Put Sts in pairs and get them to tell each other which home they prefer and why. Get feedback.

# 8A Work

## A pairwork vocabulary race
Sts read a series of clues and write the words. Copy one worksheet per pair.

### LANGUAGE
Words associated with work
- Put Sts in pairs and give out the worksheets. Set a time limit. Tell Sts that they have to write as many words as they can in the time limit. Each word begins with (or, in the case of X, includes) a different letter of the alphabet.
- Check answers. The first pair to complete all the words correctly wins, or the pair with the most correct answers at the end of the time limit wins.

### A vocabulary race
Sts read the sentences and write the phrasal verbs. Copy one worksheet per student.

- Give out the worksheets. Set a time limit, e.g., three minutes. Tell Sts that they have to read the sentences and write as many of the phrasal verbs as they can in the PHRASAL VERB column in the time limit. Point out that the first letter is given.
- Check answers. The first student to write all the phrasal verbs correctly wins, or the student with the most correct answers at the end of the time limit wins.

# 9A Word-building

## A word-building race
Sts complete the sentences with the correct noun, adjective, or adverb from the given words. Copy one worksheet per student.

### LANGUAGE
Verbs, nouns, adjectives, adverbs
- Give out the worksheets. Set a time limit, e.g., three minutes. Tell Sts that they have to complete the sentences with the correct form of the words in bold. They must write as many words as they can in the column on the right in the time limit.
- Check answers. The first student to write all the words correctly wins, or the student with the most correct answers at the end of the time limit wins.

The missing words in sentences 1–13 are nouns, which can be reviewed in the first part of Vocabulary Bank Word-building p.164. The missing words in sentences 14–20 are positive or negative adjectives and adverbs, which can be reviewed in the second part of the same Vocabulary Bank.

# INTRODUCTION

## VOCABULARY

### Classroom language

#### Asking for help
- Excuse me.
- Can / Could you say that again, please?
- Can you help me, please?
- Can I have a copy, please?
- Can you explain something, please?
- Is this right (or wrong)?
- What does ___ mean?
- How do you say ___ in English?
- How do you spell it?
- How do you pronounce it?
- Where’s the stress?
- What’s the opposite of fast?
- What’s the past tense of eat?
- What’s the difference between meet and know?

#### Apologizing / Giving reasons
- Sorry I’m late.
- I couldn’t come to class yesterday (or on Thursday). I was sick.
- I couldn’t do the homework because…
- I won’t be able to come next Monday (or next week) because…
- I have to leave early today because…

#### Working in pairs / groups
- What do we have to do?
- What did he / she say?
- We haven’t finished (yet).
- Whose turn is it?
- It’s my (or your, his, her, our, their) turn.
- What do you think?
- Do you agree?
- I agree / don’t agree (with you).

#### Leaving
- A: See you on Monday (or next Wednesday)!
B: Yes, see you!
- A: Have a good weekend!
B: You too. / The same to you.

# 6A VOCABULARY Movies

Read the movie reviews and complete them with the correct words.

## Bill’s Movie Reviews

### Dunkirk

Dunkirk is a war movie directed by Christopher Nolan, set in World War II during ___1___. Nolan has used a lot of ___2___ actors and ___3___ to make this a story about many different people, so there isn’t one ___4___ here. That being said, my favorite performance is definitely Tom Hardy’s, as a pilot who doesn’t give up even when his plane has crashed. It’s a very exciting movie, scary at times and definitely violent, so I wouldn’t recommend it for all ages. The ___5___, composed by Hans Zimmer, is really intense, and Zimmer has done an amazing job of making the music the perfect background for all the different stories. I will say that having so many characters made the ___6___ very complicated, and that made Dunkirk very difficult to follow. All in all, I enjoyed Dunkirk and I would definitely recommend it!!

----

## MOVIE Wonder Woman

I’d been looking forward to Wonder Woman since I saw the ___7___ earlier this year. It didn’t disappoint me! One of the big problems with an ___8___ movie is that usually there are a lot of ___9___ special effects and all the digital stuff can make movies look a bit silly and unreal. In this case, however, the ___10___ script is so well written that even the explosions and the superhero costumes didn’t annoy me. Gal Gadot plays Diana (they never call her Wonder Woman), a heroine who leaves her perfect island to help humans during World War II. Here, Wonder Woman becomes more of a historical movie and Diana finds the way men treat her very strange. This creates a lot of funny moments; my favorite ___11___ scene is one in which Diana complains about long skirts being really impractical to fight in! Diana tries to be both strong and kind, as she and her friends search for Ares, the God of War. The movie studio has confirmed that there will be a ___12___ sequel to Wonder Woman and I can’t wait to see it!

----

## Classic MOVIE Fan

### Seven Samurai

I had read only good things from critics about Akira Kurosawa’s classic movie Seven Samurai, so I was very excited to finally watch it. I watched it in Japanese with English ___13___ subtitles because I don’t think it has been ___14___ dubbed into English. I was very surprised by this movie. It isn’t a ___15___ comedy, but it has some very funny moments. I think I imagined it was going to be a boring ___16___ drama and instead it was moving and beautiful. Seven Samurai is ___17___ set in Japan in 1586, and tells the story of seven warriors who decide to defend a village against thieves. At first, they do it for money, but then they get to know the people in the village and they really care about them. Each character is very different, but they all become good friends and the cast does a really good job of showing you the change in their relationships. Even if you don’t like old movies, or movies in black and white, I think this is one of those special movies that everyone should watch.

This attractive and modern home has a ¹¹p __________ with amazing views of the surrounding countryside and 12st __________ down to a swimming pool. The ¹³fi __________ fl consists of a large living room, a spacious kitchen with room for a dining table, and a small bathroom. The two bedrooms and the luxurious bathroom are on the ¹⁴s __________ fl. There is also a large ¹⁵b __________ downstairs, for all your storage needs. Booking a viewing is strongly recommended, as we think it will be sold quickly!

### ACTIVATION
Look at the three homes and talk with your partner. Which one would you like to live in? Why?

# 9A VOCABULARY - Word-building

## Change the word!

Complete the sentences with the correct form of the words in bold. You can make nouns, positive or negative adjectives, and adverbs. Write your answers in the column on the right.

1. I wrote a letter of **complaint** to the hotel manager because I wasn’t happy with the service. **COMPLAIN**

2. The airline didn’t offer us any **compensation** for the ten-hour delay. **COMPENSATE**

3. Listen! The neighbors next door are having another **argument**. **ARGUE**

4. If you buy the washing machine today, we can guarantee next-day **delivery**. **DELIVER**

5. The dinner party wasn’t a **success** because Joe was rude to Richard. **SUCCEED**

6. My mom learned to swim when she was 67. I think that’s a real **achievement**. **ACHIEVE**

7. What **explanation** did Anna give you for not finishing her report? **EXPLAIN**

8. I got Tony’s email, but he forgot to send the **attachment**. **ATTACH**

9. We have an **agreement** at home that if my husband cooks, I do the dishes. **AGREE**

10. The salesperson gave me a very good **demonstration** of how the gadget worked. **DEMONSTRATE**

11. PayPal is a safe way of making a **payment** online. **PAY**

12. The company had a **loss** for five years and eventually it had to close down. **LOSE**

13. The house next door is for **sale**. I’d love to buy it! **SELL**

14. The bed was so **uncomfortable** that I wasn’t able to sleep. **COMFORT**

15. These sandals are so **comfortable**. It feels as if I’m not wearing anything on my feet. **COMFORT**

16. Having to wait for people makes me really **impatient**. **PATIENT**

17. Nick’s **unlucky** in love. His partners always leave him! **LUCK**

18. Be **careful** not to break your leg on the first day of your skiing vacation! **CARE**

19. Paolo won’t pass his English exam. He writes very **carelessly** and doesn’t check for mistakes. **CARE**

20. **Unfortunately**, it rained on our wedding day, but we had a great day anyway. **FORTUNE**

## ACTIVATION
Test your memory. Cover the column on the right. Read the sentences aloud with the correct form of the words in bold.



# VOCABULARY REVIEW

## Phrasal verbs

How many of these phrasal verbs can you remember?

| PHRASAL VERB |
|--------------|
| 1 You need to **warm** up before you do any exercise. |
| 2 My father got a loan from the bank to help **set** up the business. |
| 3 To charge your phone, attach it to the charger, **plug** it in to the outlet, and wait until it is completely charged. |
| 4 I think I need to **cut** down on soda – I drink too much. |
| 5 We often **eat** out because neither of us likes cooking. |
| 6 My doctor told me I had to **cut** out dairy products because I have an allergy. |
| 7 I need to **pay** back the money my father lent me. |
| 8 I’ll come and **pick** you up in the car at six o’clock. We can go to the party together. |
| 9 The goalkeeper was **sent** off for a bad foul. |
| 10 You seemed so happy together. Why did you **break** up? |
| 11 I need to get a better job. My salary isn’t enough to **live** on. |
| 12 The alarm went off at 7:00, but I didn’t **get** up until 8:00. |
| 13 I like to stay healthy, so I **work** out four or five times a week. |
| 14 I need to go to the cash machine and **take** out some money before we get the train. |
| 15 I really **look** forward to my weekly dance class. It’s fun! |
| 16 Small children often **fall** down when they’re learning how to walk. |
| 17 If you want to find a job, you could **look** for one on the internet or in the newspapers. |
| 18 We’ve **run** out of milk. Can you get some on your way home tonight? |
| 19 **Watch** out! You might get hit by a car. |
| 20 Can you **turn** up the music? I can’t hear it. |

## ACTIVATION
Test your memory. Cover the PHRASAL VERB column. Read the sentences aloud with the correct phrasal verbs.
